# The 1995 source and NOITE.EXE

The game disc carries an early version of the original C++ engine source. It sits in
`assets/PAN/WAV/DEMOG.ZIP` (25 Oct 1995) and `PED1.ZIP` (May–24 Oct 1995), and is unpacked
in `ref/src1995/`; `ref/src1995/README.md` has the provenance, the file list and the build
setup. This document has four parts:

- the catalogue of the source files;
- the mapping of the 1995 classes, vtables, fields, globals and functions onto NOITE.EXE,
  with a proposed name for each and a confidence;
- the behaviour the source explains, including BUGS.md;
- the minigames that share the engine.

**Nothing has been renamed in Ghidra yet.** The proposed names are for the owner to review.

## Headline

- **Debug symbols: a jackpot.** `ped1/DEMO.EXE` (linked 24 Oct 1995) and `ped1/TESTE.EXE` are
  debug builds with **full CodeView 4 (NB09)** information, and `ped1/DEMO.MAP` is the linker
  map with line numbers. They give:
  - 925 procedures with their real names and prototypes, and the names of every parameter
    and local;
  - 67 classes and structs (35 of them game classes) with member names, offsets and types;
  - every virtual method with its vtable offset.

  `tools/cv4dump.py` decodes them into `ref/src1995/symbols.txt` and `symbols-teste.txt`.
  DEMO.EXE is worth importing into Ghidra as a twin (see "Next steps").
- **This is NOITE's engine, eight months earlier.** DEMO.MAK's 15 modules are NOITE's engine
  core, and the class lattice is the same: object, animated, moveable, `_3D_*`, exits,
  portable, control_panel, status_bar, screen, occup_screen, `_3D_screen`, scene, application
  and event. NE segment 1 (the 32-bit FAST32 blitters) is byte-identical in both EXEs. The
  differences:
  - NOITE adds 10 SceneObject slots: RLE, AVI, reoccupy, the scroll target, advanceFrames,
    moveToAndDrop and the directional-player and Animated3D type tests.
  - NOITE drops one `DrawSolid` overload.
  - Fields shift by 2 after `+0x450`: NOITE packs to 2 bytes, 1995 used `/Zp4`.
  - NOITE grows Game, UpdateList and ControlPanel.
- **Coverage:**

  | Measure | Count |
  |---|---|
  | DEMO.EXE engine functions, not counting thunks and the CRT | 558 |
  | Matched to a NOITE.EXE function | 444 (80 %) |
  | Distinct NOITE functions matched | 439 |
  | NOITE engine functions (1010/1018, not thunks, handlers, CRT or API) | 654 |
  | NOITE engine functions given an original name | 433 (66 %) |

  - The rest of NOITE is code written after October 1995: the AVI and WAVE layers, RLE,
    saving, minigame plumbing, the DirectionalPlayer, and the scene handlers (`MSGHANDL.CPP`
    holds only the very first, QuartoZe's).
  - The 114 unmatched 1995 functions are mostly inline accessors that the optimiser folded
    into NOITE, sample code NOITE dropped (`DibCreate`, `DibMapToPalette`), and `object_tree`'s
    binary-tree code.
- **Matches by confidence:**

  | Confidence | Matches |
  |---|---|
  | 9 | 238 |
  | 8 | 110 |
  | 7 | 38 |
  | 6 | 37 |
  | 5 | 21 |
  | **8 or more** | **348** |

  Beyond functions:
  - all 42 vtable pairs are aligned slot by slot;
  - the field tables name every field of SceneObject, Mover, Object3D, Animation, Exit,
    Portable, ControlPanel, StatusBar, Backdrop, GridBackdrop, PerspectiveBackdrop, Scene,
    Game, DrawList and OccupancyGrid, including **22 distinct `unk*` fields**;
  - 63 globals are matched by name, among them the eight `g_iniLimits1..8`, which are the
    ceiling and the two wall triangles, and `g_iniArg816C`, which is `Cor`.

## How the mapping was made

Everything here can be rerun. The scratch scripts used `ref/src1995/ped1/DEMO.EXE`, the
tracker DB and `assets/NOITE.EXE`. `ref/src1995/mapping.tsv` is the machine-readable result.

1. **Function features** come from both EXEs' NE relocations and an `objdump` of every
   function. The features are:
   - far-call targets, both imports and internal calls;
   - virtual-call slot offsets;
   - `[bx+disp]` field offsets;
   - immediates;
   - instruction count.

   DEMO.EXE is `/Od` with `__chkstk`, and NOITE.EXE is optimised, so there is no
   byte-level matching. The score combines a Jaccard of the imports and the immediates, a
   fuzzy (±6) match of the field offsets, call-count and vcall-count ratios, and a size
   ratio. Tiny functions are down-weighted.
2. **Vtables (strongest evidence).**
   - The vtable publics (`??_7class@@6F…@`) give every 1995 vtable and its slot targets.
   - Each one is paired with its NOITE vtable (`object`→`g_sceneObjectVtbl`,
     `_3D_object`→`g_object3dVtbl`, …).
   - The 92-slot `object` table was aligned to the 102-slot SceneObject table by hand (table
     below). That alignment is then applied to all 14 SceneObject-shaped vtables.
   - Smaller tables are aligned 1:1 or by dynamic programming over the feature score.
   - vtordisp thunks are resolved on both sides (DEMO through its `JMP FAR`, NOITE through
     `absorbed_by`).
3. **Call graph.**
   - For each matched pair, the two ordered lists of internal callees are aligned.
   - Aligned callees with a score ≥ 0.5 are matched, and this repeats until nothing changes.
4. **Link order.**
   - NOITE keeps the module link order. For example, OBJECT.CPP is 1010:b072–d4f6 in the
     same order as in the source.
   - Unmatched 1995 functions that sit between two matched neighbours are aligned against
     the NOITE functions between those neighbours' addresses.
5. **Feature fallback.** A function still unmatched is matched to its best feature hit only
   when that hit scores ≥ 0.72 and beats the second-best by ≥ 0.1.
6. **Review.**
   - Conflicts, where different 1995 methods land on one NOITE function, drop to confidence 5.
   - Three matches were corrected or dropped by hand: `ReadPortable` → `ini_read_portable`,
     `object_list::Insert` → `object_list_add`, and `sprite::Frame` and `PutDIBits` dropped.

Confidence scale:

| Confidence | How the match was made |
|---|---|
| 9 | vtable slot and feature score ≥ 0.45 |
| 8 | vtable slot alone, or call-graph alignment with score ≥ 0.65 |
| 7 | call graph ≥ 0.55, or link order ≥ 0.55 |
| 6 | weaker call graph or link order, or a feature match ≥ 0.8 |
| 5 | link order < 0.45, a feature match, or a conflict |

**Proposed names** follow CLAUDE.md:
- the module's two-letter prefix, then `snake_case` of the original method;
- the class is kept when it is not the module's main class;
- `_3D_x` becomes `x3d`.

The prefixes are:

| Module | Prefix | Module | Prefix | Module | Prefix |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TBLT | `tb_` | SCREEN | `sc_` | VTREE | `vt_` |
| UTILS | `ut_` | APPLICAT | `ap_` | GENGAME | `gg_` |
| 3DOBJECT | `o3_` | EVENT | `ev_` | GENAPLIC | `ga_` |
| 3DSCREEN | `s3_` | CTRPANEL | `cp_` | GENSCENE | `gs_` |
| OBJECT | `ob_` | SCENE | `sn_` | READDATA | `rd_` |

These are mechanical proposals. A rename pass should keep the descriptive current names
where the original is vaguer, for example `Show` vs `blit_to_dc`.


## Catalogue


Paths: `ref/src1995/demog/` (25 Oct 1995, newest) and `ref/src1995/ped1/` (May-24 Oct 1995,
plus build products). Exact class layouts, vtables and procedure addresses of DEMO.EXE are in
`ref/src1995/symbols.txt` (CodeView of the ped1 build, linked 24 Oct 1995): section "Types:
classes and structs" (line ~4738) and "Procedures ... per module" (line ~105). The layouts there
are the **ped1** versions; demog differs slightly (e.g. `scene` gains `boolean Initiated`,
`_3D_player` ctor gains `_NumberOfSprites`, `gameplay.hpp`'s `_3D_game_player` is new).

Project path in the makefiles: `D:\ATR\CASSETA\APLICAT\` (NOITE.EXE's own leaked path is
`d:\atr\casseta\transrle.cpp`, same tree). Older build: `D:\LC\DEMO\` (DEMOLC.MAK).

### Builds

| Makefile | Target | Objects |
|---|---|---|
| `ped1/DEMO.MAK` (24 Oct, debug `/AL /G3 /Zp4 /vmg`, libs `libw llibcew wing wintoon commdlg mmsystem`) | DEMO.EXE | TBLT, UTILS, 3DOBJECT, 3DSCREEN, OBJECT, SCREEN, APPLICAT, EVENT, CTRPANEL, SCENE, VTREE, GENGAME, GENAPLIC, GENSCENE, READDATA + `OBJS_EXT = FAST32.OBJ` (+ DEMO.RES: HAND/LEFT/RIGHT.CUR, LIGHTON.ICO) |
| `ped1/DEMOLC.MAK` (7 May, `D:\LC\DEMO\`) | DEMO.EXE (old engine) | DEMO.CPP, TBLT.CPP, UTILS.CPP, GAMEDEFS.CPP + FAST32.OBJ |
| `ped1/TESTE.MAK` (20 Oct) | TESTE.EXE | READDATA.OBJ only (a test bed for the INI reader) |

DEMO.EXE's module table (symbols.txt "Modules", entries 84-99) has exactly the 15 DEMO.MAK
objects plus `D:\ATR\CASSETA\APLICAT\FAST32.OBJ` (NE segment 1, `0001:0000+1A5`). Not in any
build: MSGHANDL, CASSETA, SALA, BARATA, POLAR, TREATCOL, SYSTIMER, TBLT.C, TBLT2, TBLTOLD,
UTILS.C, DEMO.C, DEMO1, DEMO2, DEMOF, MECH, GAMEDEFS (only DEMOLC). Note demog's
GENAPLIC.CPP calls `QuartozeMessageHandling` (MSGHANDL.CPP), so the 25 Oct build that demog
belongs to must have added MSGHANDL.CPP to its makefile (no demog makefile survives).

Headers referenced but missing from both zips: `barata.hpp`, `brother.hpp` (BARATA.CPP),
`polar.hpp` (POLAR.CPP), `pcarnivo.hpp` (SALA.CPP). `SALA.HPP` is present but stale (it
does not declare what SALA.CPP defines).

### Scenes (SCENENUM.H, the handler table)

`SCENENUM.H` (demog): `_QUARTOZE 0, _GAVBOY1 1, _GAVBOY2 2, _GAVBOY3 3, _CORREDOR 4, _RADIO 18`
(`_RADIO` is an object number, the radio in QuartoZe, not a scene; ped1 lacks it). These are
the `Screen=<n>,...` numbers of `ped1/CASSETA.INI` ([QuartoZe] 0, [Gavboy1-3] 1-3, [Corredor]
4; it also names Sala, QuarPais, Cozinha, Prateleira, GavPais3, GavCorr, telefone not yet
defined). The shipped CASSETA.INI renumbers them (1fquarbo 45, 1fgvboy1-3 33-35, 1fcorred 30,
1fsala 47, 1fcozin0 31, 1fquarpa 46, 1fgarage 32, 1furso 49, 1fcarnbo/1fcarnch 100/101...).

The per-scene handler table is in source: `GENSCENE.CPP` defines
`boolean (*VetMessageHandling[MAX_SCENES])(HWND,UINT,WPARAM,LPARAM)` (`MAX_SCENES 200`,
GAMES.HPP), `GENAPLIC.CPP InitGame()` clears it and sets `VetMessageHandling[_QUARTOZE] =
&QuartozeMessageHandling`, and `gen_scene::MessageHandling` calls
`VetMessageHandling[SceneNumber]` first and falls back to the default code if it returns
false. `SceneNumber` is the `Screen=` number. This confirms CLAUDE.md's inferred "handler index
is the scene number".

### Engine files (in DEMO.MAK)

Class hierarchy (all far, `far pascal` methods, `/vmg` virtual inheritance):
`object` <- virtual `animated`, virtual `moveable`; `actor : moveable, animated`; `player :
actor`; `_3D_object : virtual object`; `_3D_animated : animated, virtual _3D_object`;
`_3D_moveable : moveable, virtual _3D_object`; `_3D_actor : _3D_moveable, _3D_animated`;
`_3D_player : _3D_actor`; `exits : _3D_animated`; `portable : _3D_moveable`; `control_panel :
_3D_animated`; `casseta_panel : control_panel`; `status_bar : object`; `bg_layer :
_3D_moveable`. Screens: `screen` <- `occup_screen` <- `_3D_screen` <- `parallax_screen`.
Scenes: `scene` <- `gen_scene`. Game: `application` <- `gen_applic`. These map 1:1 onto the
port's SceneObject / Animated3D / Moveable / Actor / DirectionalPlayer / Exit / Portable /
ControlPanel / StatusBar / Backdrop / Scene / Game (docs/DESIGN.md).

#### GAMES.HPP
Global header: `boolean` enum, `header`/`pal` structs, `MAX_SCENES 200`, `MAX_NAME_LEN`,
sprite timer ids, the private messages `WM_SYSTIMER 0x118`, `WM_INITSCENE..WM_PLAYERMOVE =
WM_USER+1..5`, `REFRESH_TIMER/PERIOD`, `HAS_PLAYER/NO_PLAYER`, `HAS_CTRL_PANEL`,
`HAS_STAT_BAR`, `PORTAB_DELTA_X/Y`, `GAME_CLASS gen_applic`, `extern VetMessageHandling`.
All of the game.

#### SCREEN.HPP / SCREEN.CPP
Classes `screen` (0x454: WinGDC `hBuffer`, `pBuffer`, palette, `pBack` background DIB,
buffer/screen rect, `BufferHeader`, `LocalObjects`; virtual hooks for occupancy, scrolling and
the 3-D getters `XV3D YV3D v3D DX3D xv3D yv3D zv3D`, `Set3DPosition`, `SetVanishingPoint`,
`SetViewPoint`, `SetyAxisShift`, `ScrollLayers`, `To3DScreen`...), `occup_screen : screen`
(+`Occup`), `occup_matrix` (the occupancy grid: `OccupObject`, `LastOccupObject`, `NLines`,
`NCols`). Constants: `CELL_WIDTH = CELL_HEIGHT = 16` (`H_POWER/W_POWER 4`), `MAX_X 1280`,
`MAX_Y 1024`, `MAX_OBJ_SCENE 50`, `DELTASCROLL 20`. Functions: `screen::Init` (WinGCreateDC,
WinGCreateBitmap, WinGRecommendDIBFormat), `InitBufferHeader`, `ReserveWindowsPalette`,
`FillPalette`, `TestScroll`, `Dark`, `Paint`; `occup_matrix::IncrOccupation/DecrOccupation/
Occupation/IncrOccup/DecrOccup`. The scene backdrop (port `backdrop`) and the occupancy grid
(port `grid`: 16-px cells, 65 x 81 = 1024/16+1 x 1280/16+1).

#### 3DSCREEN.HPP / 3DSCREEN.CPP
`_3D_screen : occup_screen` (0x468) adds `XV, YV` (vanishing point), `v` (eye-to-screen
distance), `DX` (x shift of the 3-D origin), `xv, yv, zv` (view point); getters, and
`SetVanishingPoint`, `SetViewPoint`, `SetyAxisShift`, `Set3DPosition(_3D_object*)`.
`parallax_screen : _3D_screen` (`VetLayers[MAX_LAYERS=5]`, `NumLayers`, `DefLayer`,
`ScrollLayers`, `PaintLayers`). The CPP also holds `bg_layer`'s methods (`Scroll`, `Paint`,
`PutIntoBuffer`, `RestoreBackGround`, `Show`, `Refresh`, `Occupy/DesoccupScreen`) and
`screen::Restore`, `screen::Show`. The perspective model of every scene: the six numbers after
the size on a CASSETA.INI `Screen=` line (vanish X/Y, view X/Y, view x/y/z, axis X/Y) feed
these three setters (GENSCENE `ReadScreen`). Parallax layers: no NOITE counterpart found in the
tracker.

#### BGLAYER.HPP
`bg_layer : _3D_moveable`: a scrolling background layer for `parallax_screen` (inline
overrides of Show/Refresh/Paint/PutIntoBuffer/RestoreBackGround/Occupy). Used by 3DSCREEN only.

#### OBJECT.HPP / OBJECT.CPP
`frame` (bounds), `sprite` (0x1C: frames, frame size, count, `pBitMap` DIB, bounds; `Init`,
`Frame`, `FrameX/Y`), `object_list` (linked list: `FirstObject`, counts; `Insert`, `Exclude`,
`Contains`, `FreeAll`, `Put`, `Restore`, `Init`, `IsEmpty`, `IsFull`), `object` (0x490: list
links, `Layer`, `X/Y`, `Name` (= object number), palette offset, `Sprites`, current
sprite/frame, `Orientation`, visibility/initialised flags, collision state, dirty rects,
`_SceneName`; ~90 virtuals: the whole SceneObject interface incl. the 3-D getters, draw/save/
restore, Occupy/DesoccupScreen, anim and move entry points, Is*/To* type tests, IsClass1..5),
`animated : virtual object` (`Timer2`, `Period2`, `FrameCounter`, `_1SpriteAnim`,
`EndsAtFirstFrame`, `Animating`; NextFrame/InitAnim/Anim1Frame/EndAnim/StopAnim),
`moveable : virtual object` (speeds, limits, Old/New/Delta, Bresenham-style `IncrNE/IncrE/
IncrN`, `Timer1`, `Period`, `Moving`; InitMove/InitHMove/InitVMove/Move1Step/EndMove,
`PlanNextPosition`, `FinishedMovement`), `actor : moveable, animated`, `player : actor`
(`RemainningLifes`, `Score`), `object_bag` (fixed array of objects; Insert/Reset/Refresh).
OBJECT.CPP: drawing (`Show`, `Refresh`, `SaveBackGround`, `RestoreBackGround`,
`PutIntoBuffer`, `PutDirtyIntoBuffer`, `UpdatePalette`), collision (`DetectedCollision`,
`DetectedFrameCollision`, `DetectedDirtyCollision`, `TreatCollision`), movement and animation
bodies, `object_list` methods; free functions `NewName`, `AddPointer`. Port: `render`,
`movement`, `animation`, `object_list`, `update_list` units.

#### 3DOBJECT.HPP / 3DOBJECT.CPP (the largest module, 0x35D0 bytes code)
`_3D_object : virtual object` (0x4B0: `x, y, z` world position, `w, h, d` box, `X3DO, Y3DO`
anchor in the sprite, `UnderObj`; `x3D..d3D`, `Set3DPosition`, `Set3DOrigin`,
`Set3DDimensions`, `SetZ0`, `Find_z`, `IsOn`, `IsOver`, `IsCloserThan`, `DetectedCollision`,
`TreatCollision`, `DrawSolid` (debug wire box)), `_3D_animated`, `_3D_moveable`, `_3D_actor`
(+`Portables` list), `_3D_player` (+lives, score; Occupy/DesoccupScreen), `exits :
_3D_animated` (0x514: target scene name, player position, has-player/panel/bar flags,
click-exit, 1-animation, `OpenningEvent`, `ExitNum`, `KeyObject`, alternative exit; `HasKey`,
`InitAnim`, `Anim1Frame`, `InitExit`, `SetAlternativeExit`, `SetExitNum`), `portable :
_3D_moveable` (0x500: `Owner`, `pIcon`, `IconName`, icon size, `HasBeenTouched`;
`ChangeOwner(actor|scene)`, `PutIcon`, `HideIcon`, `DefIcon`, `SetZ0`). Free functions
`Get3DPosition(screen, &x, &y, z, X, Y, w)` (screen -> ground plane) and
`Get2DPosition(screen, x, y, z, &X, &Y, w)` (world -> screen: `X = x + (XV - x)*y/(y + v)`,
`Y = H - (z + (YV - z)*y/(y + v))`). These are NOITE's `perspective_unproject_point`
1010:4836 / `perspective_project_point` 1010:4964 (port `src/projection.zig`). Most of the
587 procedures in the module are compiler-generated vtable/vbase thunks.

#### VTREE.H / VTREE.CPP
`object_node` (`Obj`, `l`, `r`), `object_tree` (binary tree of `MAX_VIS_OBJ = 100` nodes;
Insert/Extract/Show in-order) and `object_ordlist` (the same nodes as an ordered list: `List[100]`,
`New`, `First`; Insert by depth / Extract / Show). The back-to-front draw order of the 3-D
objects. NOITE's `draw_list` (1018:2b1a-2e88, "back-to-front draw list of 3D objects (100-node
pool)") is `object_ordlist`.

#### SCENE.HPP / SCENE.CPP
`scene` (0x64: `Screen`, `VetExits`, `NumExits`, `Name`, `LocalObjects`, has-player/panel/bar
flags, `Is1Animation`, `IsClickExit`, `AutoExit`, `MayDropObjects`, `SceneNumber`; demog adds
`Initiated`; virtuals `MessageHandling`, `HasPlayer..`, `DefLocal`, `CreateScreen`,
`CreateNewScene`, `Init`, `Show`, `Dark`, `Paint`, `DefExit`, `Exit`) and `gen_scene : scene`
(the data-driven scene). SCENE.CPP: the `scene` methods, `exits::InitExit`, and the
`screen` default virtuals. Port: `scene`.

#### GENSCENE.CPP
`DefaultMessageHandling`, the `VetMessageHandling[200]` table, `gen_scene` (ctor reads the
INI section; `Init` reads `Screen=` (number, bmp, rect, perspective), `Limits=`, then a loop on
the line's first letter: `L`ocal -> `_3D_object`, `S`tatic, `A`nimated -> `_3D_animated`,
`E`xit -> `exits`; `MessageHandling` = scene handler first, then `WM_INITSCENE`, mouse clicks,
`WM_SYSTIMER` animation ticks, `WM_EXITSCENE`; `CreateNewScene`, `DefExit`, `DefAltExit`). Also
hosts out-of-line ctors (`_3D_screen`, `occup_screen`, `occup_matrix`, `screen`, `exits`,
`scene`, `animated`) and `application::DefStatic`. NOITE's scene loader (`scene_loader`,
`ini_readers`). Generic; covers every scene defined in CASSETA.INI.

#### READDATA.HPP / READDATA.CPP
C-style CASSETA.INI parser (Portuguese names): `Error`, `BuildSectionIndex`,
`FindSectionOffset`, `SkipSeparators`, `Comment`, `SkipLine`, `SkipChar`, `ReadStr`,
`SearchLine`, `AbreArquivo` (open), `FechaArquivo` (close), `SeekScene`, `MontaNome` (build
BMP name + index), `LookChar`, `ReadPlayer`, `ReadSprite`, `ReadControlPanel`,
`ReadStatusBar`, `ReadFirstScene`, `ReadPortable`, `ReadLimits`, `ReadScreen`, `ReadObject`,
`ReadAnimated`, `ReadStatic`, `ReadExit`, `SavePos`, `RestPos`; struct `SectionList`
(section name/offset index). Port: `ini`, `ini_sections`, `ini_readers`.

#### APPLICAT.HPP / APPLICAT.CPP
`application` (0x564: `CurrentScene`, `LastScene`, `StatusBar`, `ControlPanel`, `ObjectList`,
`Player`, `Portables`, `FirstStaticObject`, `DirtyObjects`, `VisibleObjects`, default screen
type/size; virtuals `MessageHandling`, `CreatePlayer`, `CreateInitScene`, `CreateControlPanel`,
`CreateStatusBar`, `Init`) and `gen_applic : application`. APPLICAT.CPP:
`application::MessageHandling` (the engine default: scene handler via `CurrentScene`, arrow
keys walk the player, `WM_LBUTTONDOWN` walk, `WM_MOUSEMOVE` cursors, `WM_SYSTIMER` move/anim
ticks and panel slide, `WM_RBUTTONDOWN/UP` pick up / drop portables, `WM_EXITSCENE`),
`SetCurrentScene` (scene change). Port: `game`, `game_input`, `game_scenes`.

#### GENAPLIC.CPP
`gen_applic` methods that build the game from CASSETA.INI's `[Casseta]` section:
`CreatePlayer` (`Player=` + `Sprite=` lines -> `_3D_game_player`), `CreateControlPanel`,
`CreateStatusBar`, `CreateInitScene` (`FirstScene=` -> `gen_scene`), `Init` (opens
casseta.ini, the `Portable=` loop -> `portable` + `DefIcon`, posts `WM_INITSCENE`); `InitGame`
(fills `VetMessageHandling`); globals for every INI field (`VanishX`, `ViewX`, `AxisX`,
`LinhaSup`...). Also out-of-line ctors (`_3D_player`, `control_panel`, `status_bar`,
`application`, `object`...). Port: `object_create`, `game`.

#### GENGAME.CPP
The WinG-sample window shell ("Generic Game"): `AppAbout`, `AppInit` (ClearSystemPalette,
class, `InitGame`, `Game = new GAME_CLASS`, CreateWindow, single-instance check "O aplicativo
ja esta aberto"), `AppExit`, `MessageLoop`, `WinMain`, `AppIdle`, `AppPaint`, `AppWndProc`
(forwards everything to `Game->MessageHandling`), `AppCommand`, a printf-style `MessageBox`;
`SetSystemTimer`/`KillSystemTimer` fetched from USER ordinals 11/182. Port: `app`.

#### CTRPANEL.HPP / CTRPANEL.CPP
`control_panel : _3D_animated` (0x59C: `On`, `Direction`, `AnimCounter`, `TextColor`,
`Buffer[200]`, `Pos`; `IsOn`, `PanelHeight`, `PutObjects`, `IsOut`, `IsIn`, `SelectedObject`,
`PutIcon`, `Constr`, Set/Reset/Update text), `casseta_panel : control_panel` (the game's
inventory panel: `PutIcon`, `SelectedObject`, `PanelHeight`), `status_bar : object` (0x560:
text colour, buffer; `SetText`, `ResetText`, `Update`). demog adds an unused `struct
ctrl_panel`. Port: `panels`.

#### EVENT.HPP / EVENT.CPP
`event` (virtual `HasOccurred`, `Occur`, `Reset`; `operator&&`, `operator||`),
`primitive_event : event` (`State`), `compound_event : event` (`e1`, `e2`, `oper`). Used as
an exit's opening condition (`exits::OpenningEvent`). Port: `condition` (Condition /
FlagCondition / CompoundCondition, 1010:0a4e-0abc).

#### TBLT.H / TBLT.CPP (in build; FIRSTCPP)
WinG sample's transparent blt, C++ with `extern "C"`: `TranspDIBits` (clip + call
`TransCopyDIBBits`, with width/height), `TransparentDIBits`, `PutDIBits`, `CopyDIBits`
(clip + call `CopyDIBBits`). NOITE has two: `dib_copy_transparent` 1010:0000 = `TranspDIBits`
(same 12 parameters: dst header/bits, dst x/y, src bits/header, src x/y, width, height,
iUsage, key) and `dib_copy` 1010:01e0 = `CopyDIBits` (9 parameters; the port's
`unused1..3` are nXOriginSrc, nYOriginSrc, iUsage). Port: `dib`.

#### UTILS.H / UTILS.CPP (in build)
WinG sample DIB helpers: `ClearSystemPalette`, `DibOpenFile`, `DibReadBitmapInfo`,
`DibSetUsage`, `DibCreate`, `DibMapToPalette`, static `xlatClut8/4`, `xlatRle8/4`, `hmemmove`;
the Dib* macros. NOITE's 1010:038c `clear_system_palette`, 041c `load_dib` (DibOpenFile), 05d8
`read_dib_info` (DibReadBitmapInfo), 0832 `dib_remap8` (xlatClut8), 088c `dib_remap4`, 08fc
`dib_remap_rle8`, 09c8 `FUN_1010_09c8` (8 bytes = `xlatRle4`, empty), 09d0 `hmemmove_backward`
(hmemmove), in the same order right after TBLT's two functions; DibSetUsage, DibCreate and
DibMapToPalette are not in NOITE. `UTILS.C` is the identical C original (not built).

#### FAST32.ASM (ped1 only; FAST32.OBJ in OBJS_EXT) and FAST32CP.ASM
Microsoft WinG sample "32 bit code segment version of FAST16.ASM" (cmacro32.inc,
`createSeg FAST_TEXT32 ... use32`): `TransCopyDIBBits` and `CopyDIBBits`, far pascal,
unrolled-by-4 byte loops. FAST32CP.ASM is the same with C++-decorated names
(`?TransCopyDIBBits@@ZCXGKPFXKKJJE@Z`, `?CopyDIBBits@@ZCXGKPFXKKJJ@Z`), not used. This is NE
segment 1 of both DEMO.EXE and NOITE.EXE (see answer 2).

### Answer 1: 3DOBJECT / 3DSCREEN / RAY.H / VTREE / BGLAYER vs JARDIM's raycaster

Confirmed: "3D" in these files is NOITE's 2.5-D perspective scene, not a raycaster.

- `_3D_screen` is a single-vanishing-point projection: `XV, YV`, `v` (eye distance), `DX`,
  `xv, yv, zv`; `Get2DPosition` is `X = x + (XV - x)*y/(y + v)`, `Y = H - (z + (YV - z)*y/(y +
  v))`. These are exactly NOITE's `perspective_project_point`/`unproject_point` (1010:4964,
  1010:4836; `src/projection.zig`: "through the backdrop's vanishing point", getters
  `getVanishX/Y`, `getEyeDistance`, `getAxisX`), parameterised per scene by CASSETA.INI's
  `Screen=` fields (vanish, view point, axis shift). `_3D_object` (x,y,z,w,h,d box + sprite
  anchor) is the port's Object3D; `Find_z`/`IsOn`/`IsOver`/`IsCloserThan` give support and
  depth order; `occup_matrix` is the 16-px occupancy grid (65 x 81, `grid`); VTREE's
  `object_ordlist` (100 nodes) is the back-to-front `draw_list` (1018:2b1a-2e88).
  `parallax_screen`/`bg_layer` are scrolling background layers (unrelated to raycasting; not
  identified in NOITE).
- JARDIM's raycaster (docs/jardim/GAME.md "The raycaster", DESIGN.md) is a different thing:
  a 57 x 80 cell map (`raymapN.dat`), positions as longs in 1/32 cell, `viewAngle` with 2880
  steps per turn (view 480 = 60 degrees), seven 16.16 trig tables (tan, cot, x/y steps, the
  fisheye 1/cos table [4] for -30..+30, 1/cos, 1/sin), two 32-bit DDA walks
  (`raycast_walk_horizontal/vertical`), 4-px wall columns and a depth-keyed sprite queue,
  in a `LabyrinthBackdrop` (JARDIM 1028). None of these names, constants or tables occur in
  3DOBJECT/3DSCREEN/VTREE/BGLAYER. JARDIM still inherits NOITE's perspective Backdrop but its
  getters are constant 0 there (jardim DESIGN.md: "The Backdrop's perspective getters stay
  constants in `space3d`"): the 2.5-D model is present but unused in the labyrinth.
- The one link is `RAY.H`: `class ray { int row, column, block, t, done; long x, y,
  view_angle, x_cell, y_cell, x_sub_cell, y_sub_cell; float dx, dy; Init(); Color;
  MessageHandling(); }` (ped1 date 20 Jun 1995). That is raycaster vocabulary (cell + sub-cell
  position, view angle), matching JARDIM's model (cell = pos >> 5, sub-cell = pos & 31,
  `viewAngle`); the same naming is found in the 1994 "Tricks of the Game Programming Gurus"
  ray caster, whose table scheme (angles = view width x 6 per turn, a 3.272e-4 rad offset,
  seven tables incl. a 1/cos fisheye table) JARDIM reproduces (2880 = 480 x 6, offset
  0.0003272). That attribution is from memory, not checked against the book. RAY.H is an
  unused June 1995 placeholder, perhaps the first idea of the later `d:\c&p\doom\` program;
  it has no code here.

### Answer 2: are TBLT / FAST32 / MECH the "transrle" blitters of segment 1000?

FAST32.ASM is NOITE's segment 1000; TBLT.CPP is its 16-bit caller (in 1010); MECH is
unrelated; and the name "transrle.cpp" does not belong to segment 1000.

- Byte comparison: NE segment 1 of NOITE.EXE and of ped1/DEMO.EXE are
  **byte-identical**: 420 bytes (0x1a4 in the segment table, 0x1A5 in DEMO.MAP), flags 0x2c50
  (the 32-bit bit 0x2000 set). DEMO.MAP: `FAST_TEXT32`, `TRANSCOPYDIBBITS` at 0001:0024,
  `COPYDIBBITS` at 0001:0114 = NOITE `dib_rows_copy_transparent32` 1000:0024 and
  `dib_rows_copy32` 1000:0114. The minigames' segment 1000 is the same object (repo QUALITY
  docs: byte-identical in TIRO/ENCAIXE/INV/INFERNO/SERIAL/SALGIN/XILINDRO; JARDIM/SAUNA add a
  routine in front).
- The "16-bit mode check + DPMI self-switch" is cmacro32.inc's `cProc` prologue for a USE32
  segment, not hand-written code: each entry starts `33 C0 B4 80 03 C0 72 D4` (`xor ax,ax;
  mov ah,80h; add ax,ax; jc 0000`). In 16-bit mode the add of 0x8000 carries and jumps to the
  shared stub at 0000-0023, which calls DPMI `INT 31h` AX=000Bh (get descriptor of CS),
  sets bit 0x40 (D, default 32-bit) in byte 6, AX=000Ch (set descriptor) and returns to
  re-execute the check, which now runs as 32-bit (`xor eax,eax; mov ah,80h; add eax,eax`: no
  carry). The routine bodies are the WinG sample's loops verbatim.
- Side effect explaining port bug B12 (`src/dib.zig`): that prologue leaves EAX = 0x10000,
  so AH = 0; `CopyDIBBits`' odd-pixel tail loop is a copy-paste of the transparent one (`cmp
  al, ah; je`), so pixels of value 0 in the last `width & 3` columns are skipped. The source
  shows the copy-paste directly (FAST32.ASM, `tcdb_oddloo2`).
- TBLT.CPP is the 16-bit clip-and-call layer: NOITE `dib_copy_transparent` 1010:0000 =
  `TranspDIBits`, `dib_copy` 1010:01e0 = `CopyDIBits` (same parameter lists), followed by
  UTILS.CPP's functions in source order. TBLT.C/TBLT2/TBLTOLD are older copies. MECH.CPP is a
  "mech" walking-sprite test on the old GAMEDEFS engine, not a blitter.
- "transrle.cpp": NOITE contains the string `d:\atr\casseta\transrle.cpp` followed by
  `pOutputBuffer!= NULL` at 1018:baa0/babc (file offset 0x1ee50), right after
  `rle_blit_transparent` 1018:b78a (ends at ba9d) and before `wave_mix_init`; no code
  references them (checked the tracker's disassembly). In JARDIM/SAUNA the same assert strings
  belong to the 16-bit RLE module (`1088`: `rle_blit_transparent`, `dead_rle_encode_dib`,
  paths `d:\c&p\doom\transrle.cpp`, `d:\c&p\pacman\transrle.cpp`). So transrle.cpp is a later
  (post-Oct-1995) 16-bit RLE sprite module, of which NOITE keeps `rle_blit_transparent` in
  segment 1018 plus the dead assert strings. There is no RLE code in these sources. CLAUDE.md
  and the minigame QUALITY docs that call segment 1000 "the transrle.cpp blitters" should say
  "FAST32.ASM (WinG sample, segment FAST_TEXT32)". NOITE's `dib` unit header ("8-bit row
  blitters (32-bit segment) and the RLE sprite blitter") already covers both.

## Behaviour the source explains

Sources: `ref/src1995/demog/` (25 Oct 1995, newest) and `ped1/` (slightly older; `DEMO.MAK`
links APPLICAT, GENAPLIC, GENGAME, GENSCENE, READDATA, OBJECT, 3DOBJECT, SCREEN, 3DSCREEN, CTRPANEL, VTREE,
SCENE, EVENT, TBLT, UTILS, so DEMO.EXE is the INI-driven engine), `symbols.txt` (CodeView of DEMO.EXE,
24 Oct 1995). The files are CP1252 with CRLF. `file:line` below refers to `demog/` unless it says `ped1/`.

Class map (1995 name → port/Ghidra name): `object` → SceneObject, `moveable` → Mover, `animated` → Animation,
`_3D_object` → Object3D, `_3D_moveable` → Moveable, `_3D_animated` → Animated3D, `actor`/`player` → Actor2D/Player2D,
`_3D_actor` → Actor, `_3D_player`/`_3D_game_player` → (Directional)Player, `exits` → Exit, `portable`, `control_panel`
(`casseta_panel`), `status_bar`, `screen`/`occup_screen`/`_3D_screen` → Backdrop/GridBackdrop/PerspectiveBackdrop,
`occup_matrix` → OccupancyGrid, `object_ordlist` (`Game->VisibleObjects`) → DrawList, `object_bag`
(`Game->DirtyObjects`) → UpdateList, `object_list` → ObjectList, `application`/`gen_applic`/`casseta` → Game/NoiteGame,
`scene`/`gen_scene` → Scene/INI scene, `event`/`primitive_event`/`compound_event` → Condition/FlagCondition/CompoundCondition.


### Top 10 insights

1. **The SceneObject vtable is 1995's `object` vtable, nearly slot for slot** (`symbols.txt:5085-5205`). This gives
   the original names of the port's slots. NOITE has 4 extra slots in front (00, 01, enableRle, isRle), so 1995
   slot n is NOITE slot n+4 until `DrawSolid(hdc)` was dropped, n+3 after that, and NOITE inserts reoccupy (29),
   isAvi (2D), advanceFrames (36) and moveToAndDrop (40). Useful renames:
   - 0D/0E `getX`/`getY` = `_OldX`/`_OldY`. They return the **previous** position, which matches the port
     (`mover.prevX`).
   - 11 (dead) = `Find_z`. It moved to Object3DVtbl as findSupport.
   - 15 setWorldElev = `SetZ0`.
   - 16 drawBounds = `DrawSolid`, the `#ifdef Debug` yellow wireframe of the 3D box (`3DOBJECT.CPP:84-152`).
   - 1F slot1F = `Save` → `SaveBackGround(HDC)`, whose body is commented out (`OBJECT.CPP:93-97`). This is
     B32's "debug no-op".
   - 24 slot24 (dead) = `Refresh`.
   - 2A attach = `Init(screen)`.
   - 2C isStepBlocked = `CantGo`.
   - 31 playIdle = `InitAnimP`.
   - 5B/5C = `CollisionHandling` ×2.
   - 5F isEnabled = `IsOpened`.
   - 61-65 (dead) = `IsClass1..5`, the per-game type-test hooks that the minigames fill in.

   Unknown fields named by the symbols:
   - SceneObject+0x10 = `Layer`.
   - +0x18/+0x1a = `PaletteOffSet`/`NumColors`, and the Backdrop's `unk1E/unk20` are the same pair.
   - Mover+0x8..0x14 = `XSpeed, YSpeed, Direction, XMax, XMin, YMax, YMin`.
   - Mover+0x28 = `Counter`.
   - `InCollision` is the minigames' `unk452/45E/466`.
2. **B32 is a NOITE regression.** In 1995 a scene's sprite timers (ids `FIRST_SPRITE_TIMER` 5 to `LAST_SPRITE_TIMER`
   10, `GAMES.HPP:55-56`) tick the animations, and the scene handler then returns false (`GENSCENE.CPP:375-409`).
   `application::MessageHandling` then puts the control panel and status bar into the buffer *before* it flushes
   the dirty bag (`APPLICAT.CPP:270-287`), so the panel is never cut out. NOITE flushes inside
   `ini_scene_handle_message`, before the panel is drawn. `Fixes.bars_over_scene_animation` restores the 1995
   order. Someone also tried redrawing the panel from collision handling and commented it out
   (`3DOBJECT.CPP:703-706`).
3. **The occupancy grid encodes walls as negative counts, so L11 is half deliberate.**
   - `WALL` is -1 (`SCREEN.HPP:29`).
   - `IncrOccup` decrements a negative cell (`SCREEN.HPP:77-91`), so a wall cell holding n occupants reads -1-n.
   - `Occupation` decodes it as `maxocc = -occ-1` (`SCREEN.CPP:118-122`).
   - So `DecrOccup`'s `else ++` (`SCREEN.HPP:95-108`, with `//return WALL;` commented out) is intended for wall
     cells. Only the 0 → 1 case is the slip.

   B29 is in the 1995 code too: there is one `Obj[][]` pointer per cell, and `DecrOccup` sets it to NULL before
   the count step. The authors knew about it: `OBJECT.CPP:602-603` reads "procurar quais os objetos que ocupam a
   posicão e verificar se algum colide com this" ("find which objects occupy the position and check whether any
   collides with this"). L7's limits come from `MAX_Y 1024` / `MAX_X 1280` (`SCREEN.HPP:20-24`). The overflow
   message box is followed by no return there either (`SCREEN.HPP:132-140`).
4. **The drop logic of B25, B30, B31, B36 and B43 is already in 1995** (`APPLICAT.CPP:371-441`).
   - `Find_z` takes the first visible local, then portable, that is `IsOver` (horizontal overlap and a strictly
     higher top) or overlapping (`3DOBJECT.CPP:511-542`).
   - When the item overlaps that object it stays where it is (`Selected = NULL`).
   - Otherwise it walks to the middle of the object's projected top face plus half its height. With no support it
     walks to `ScreenHeight-100`.
   - The x passed to `InitMove` is `_X - FrameWidth/2` (`:396`), and `InitMove` subtracts `FrameWidth/2` again
     (`OBJECT.CPP:794-798`). This is B36's double half-width.
   - INI portables are built with `PORTAB_DELTA_X 0` / `PORTAB_DELTA_Y 10` (`GAMES.HPP:81-82`,
     `3DOBJECT.CPP:1080`), and `FinishedMovement` tests only x on an x-major line (`OBJECT.CPP:665-675`). Together
     these give B36's endless fall. The hand-made 1995 portables used `PORTAB_DELTAX 10` (`CASSETA.HPP:18`,
     `CASSETA.CPP:47-57`), so a step of 0 looks like a slip of the generic constructor.
   - The comment at `:426-427` reads "it is not necessary to treat collision here because the portable object will
     always be in front of any object". That is exactly the assumption B31 breaks.
   - `Selected` (the falling item) is a function static that `SetCurrentScene` never clears. That is B43.
5. **B52 is half a regression.** `object::RestoreBackGround` restores with an opaque `PutDIBits`, and the
   transparent `TranspDIBits` version sits commented out just above it (`OBJECT.CPP:152-165`).
   `moveable::RestoreBackGround` already used `TranspDIBits(..., TransparentColor)` (`OBJECT.CPP:219-224`).
   - The player's 1-px column (a moveable) is inherited from 1995.
   - NOITE's `scene_object_restore_background` going transparent is new.
   - The key is `TransparentColor = 11; //6` (`GAMES.HPP:43`; the older `GAMEDEFS.HPP:55` has 10): the key index
     changed at least twice, which also explains B53's stray index-11 pixels in the art.
6. **B12 and B13 come from Microsoft's sample code.** `ped1/FAST32CP.ASM` / `FAST32.ASM` are the WinG sample
   (`(C) Copyright 1994 Microsoft Corp.`, `:11`). `CopyDIBBits` is a copy of `TransCopyDIBBits` and keeps its
   odd-pixel tail `cmp al, ah / je` (`FAST32CP.ASM:327-337`). The copy never sets AH, and its loop comment still
   says "non transparent pixel" (`:286-287`). The two 32-bit routines in segment 1000 are this pair.
   `DibOpenFile` (`UTILS.CPP:143-146`, the SDK DIB sample) returns NULL after `DibReadBitmapInfo` fails without
   calling `_lclose`. That is B13 word for word.
7. **The engine's message, timer and exit plumbing is named.** In `GAMES.HPP:58-67`:
   - `WM_SYSTIMER` is 0x118.
   - 0x401 is `WM_INITSCENE`.
   - 0x402 is `WM_EXITSCENE`, whose comment at `GENSCENE.CPP:412` reads "wParam is the number of the exit".
   - 0x403 is `WM_ENDANIM`, 0x404 `WM_ENDMOVE`, 0x405 `WM_PLAYERMOVE`.
   - `REFRESH_TIMER` 1 runs at 50 ms.

   `SetSystemTimer`/`KillSystemTimer` are USER ordinals 11 and 182, fetched with `GetProcAddress`
   (`GENGAME.CPP:266-269`).

   B21 and B37 are the 1995 design:
   - An exit posts its *index* (`3DOBJECT.CPP:1020-1028`).
   - Two **static** `Exiting` flags gate 0x402 (`APPLICAT.CPP:32,446`; `GENSCENE.CPP:310,414`). The new scene's
     `WM_INITSCENE` re-arms one for a 1Animation scene (`:335-339`).
   - The AutoExit ticks on any `wParam == AutoExit->Timer()` (`:370-373`).
   - `~scene` kills no timers and never deletes `AutoExit`, which is not in `LocalObjects` (`SCENE.CPP:37-52`).
     That is B14.
8. **Unfinished features had working 1995 versions.**
   - **U2:** on `WM_INITSCENE` the panel's message line was set to the scene name
     (`Game->ControlPanel->SetText(Name)`, `GENSCENE.CPP:331`; the hand-written scenes used "Quarto do Ze Mane",
     `QUARTOZE.HPP:188`). `control_panel::Put` drew it in yellow at `460-PanelHeight()+20`
     (`CTRPANEL.CPP:100-167`).
   - **B27:** `PanelHeight()` is `40 + CurrentFrame*20` (`CTRPANEL.HPP:129-132`), the same formula as
     `control_panel_get_visible_height`. Its 20-px steps were made for that message line, not for the lives and
     score art NOITE added later.
   - **U1:** `status_bar::Update` exists but nothing calls it, even in 1995. `status_bar` has no `Put` override.
     Its only text was the debug coordinates from `_3D_moveable::Show` (`3DOBJECT.CPP:409-419`).
   - **U3:** the PatBlt uses `DefaultScreenWidth/Height`, and its setter `SetScreenDimensions` is never called
     (`APPLICAT.HPP:114,162`), so U3 is already in 1995.
9. **The latent draw-list and buffer bugs are explained.**
   - **L4:** `draw_list_invalidate_nodes` is `object_tree::Show` (`VTREE.CPP:202-214`). It recurses into `r` and
     `l` and then clears them, which is correct on the binary *tree* the draw list used to be. It became infinite
     recursion when the same code was kept for the doubly linked `object_ordlist`, where `l` is the previous node.
   - **L5:** `object_ordlist::Extract` does `New->l = Node` without testing `New` (`VTREE.CPP:369-374`).
   - **L6:** `MessageBox` uses `static char Out[100]` and an unbounded `sprintf` (`GENGAME.CPP:470-477`). The
     same 111-byte "...Aumente o valor da constante MAX_OBJECT_QTY" text exists (`OBJECT.HPP:1828`).
   - **L9:** `gen_scene::CreateNewScene` tests `ExitNum <= NumExits` (`GENSCENE.CPP:58`), while `scene::Exit()`
     uses `<` (`SCENE.HPP:199-202`). The inconsistency points to a slip.
   - **L10:** `SetViewPoint` and `SetyAxisShift` divide unchecked (`3DSCREEN.CPP:26-46`).
10. **The geometry of the INI keys is named and commented.**
    - `Limits=` is `LinhaSup, ColunaSup` (the ceiling, "teto"), `LinhaLatEsq, ColunaLatEsq, DeltaLatEsq` (the
      left wall triangle, "triangulo esquerdo") and `LinhaLatDir, ColunaLatDir, DeltaLatDir` (the right one). The
      right wall runs to a hard-coded column 40 (`GENSCENE.CPP:109-135`), as the port does (`0x28`). The port could
      take these names for `r.limits[0..7]`.
    - `Screen=` holds the vanishing point (`XV, YV`), the view point (`v` = distance from the viewpoint to the
      screen; `xv, yv, zv` = its 3D coordinates) and the y-axis shift (`DX`, "correction of the x coordinate when
      the 3D origin is shifted", `3DSCREEN.HPP:14-20`). An older comment says `v` was "assumed 3*ScreenWidth"
      (`GAMEDEFS.HPP:443`).
    - `_3DOriginX/Y` is "the Botton-Left-Front corner" *in the frame* (`3DOBJECT.HPP:22`), or "1 par front inf esq
      do frame, 2 par dimensões ... da caixa" ("first pair = the frame's front lower-left corner, second pair = the
      box's dimensions", `SALA.CPP:97`). That is the convention B30 and B40 found misapplied in the shipped INI.



### BUGS.md entries vs 1995 source

Verdicts:
- **intended:** the source shows deliberate design.
- **slip:** the source shows it is an accident: a 1995 version that was right, a contradicting comment, or a
  copy-paste.
- **same-in-1995:** the same code exists, with no sign of intent either way.
- **not-covered:** 1995 has no such code (no handlers for the shipped scenes, no sound, AVI, saves, minigames,
  RLE or score display).

| Entry | Title (short) | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1-B11, B15-B18, B20, B22-B24, B34, B38, B40-B42, B44-B47, B51 | handler, data, MCI, save and minigame bugs | not-covered | 1995 has handlers only for the old scenes (sala, barata, polar, quartoze, quarpais). No WAVE, AVI, save, lives or minigame code. |
| B12 | opaque row copy skips index 0 in the tail | same-in-1995 (inherited sample code) | `ped1/FAST32CP.ASM:327-337` `cmp al, ah / je` in `CopyDIBBits`; AH is never loaded; the file is Microsoft's 1994 WinG sample. |
| B13 | non-DIB file stays open | same-in-1995 (inherited sample code) | `UTILS.CPP:143-146` `if (!pdib) return NULL;` before `_lclose(fh)` at `:170`. |
| B14 | cutscene animation leaks | same-in-1995 | `~scene` deletes `LocalObjects` and `Screen` only (`SCENE.CPP:37-52`); `AutoExit` is only `DefExit`ed (`GENSCENE.CPP:148-156`). |
| B19 | 32-bit score shown and saved as 16-bit | slip (the type was meant to be 32-bit) | `long Score` in `_3D_player`, `player` and `actor` (`3DOBJECT.HPP:631`, `OBJECT.HPP:1724`). 1995 never displays it. |
| B21 | stale animation timer takes a video scene's exit | same-in-1995 | AutoExit ticked on any matching timer id (`GENSCENE.CPP:370-373`); no timer killed on scene delete (`SCENE.CPP:37-52`); a missing section is not fatal in `SeekScene` (`READDATA.CPP:375-389`) and `gen_scene::Init` ignores its result (`GENSCENE.CPP:89`). |
| B25 | dropped item leaves its image when picked up | same-in-1995 | `moveable::SetPosition` keeps the old X/Y as `OldX/OldY` (`OBJECT.HPP:1447-1455`); only `EndMove` syncs them (`OBJECT.CPP:1003-1004`); the drop uses `SetPosition` (`APPLICAT.CPP:389`). |
| B26 | walk continues into the next scene | same-in-1995 | `SetCurrentScene` does `SetPosition` + `Appear` + `InitAnimP` with no `EndMove` (`APPLICAT.CPP:544-565`). |
| B27 | lives and score drift as the panel slides | same-in-1995 (formula built for another text) | `PanelHeight() = 40 + CurrentFrame*20` (`CTRPANEL.HPP:129-132`), used for the message line (`CTRPANEL.CPP:159`). |
| B28 | door sounds from too far | not-covered (no sound); the reach test is the same | `abs(centre difference) < 3*Player->BoundWidth()/2` (`APPLICAT.CPP:325`). The same test for portables and animations is commented out (`:339`, `:360`): reach was deliberately dropped for them. `MIN_EXIT_DIST 100` (`APPLICAT.HPP:10`) is defined and unused. |
| B29 | picked item leaves the item under it unclickable | same-in-1995, a known limitation | One `Obj[][]` per cell; `DecrOccup` sets NULL (`SCREEN.HPP:95-97`); TODO at `OBJECT.CPP:602-603`. In 1995 `moveable::CanGo` treats "count > 0, object NULL" as passable (`OBJECT.CPP:595-609`). |
| B30 | dropped item hangs in mid-air or rises | intended | `Find_z` is generic by design: the first visible object that `IsOver` or collides (`3DOBJECT.CPP:511-542`). Overlap means "already on it" (`Selected = NULL`), and the target is the top-face middle (`APPLICAT.CPP:400-424`). |
| B31 | package cut by the table | slip (a stated assumption that fails) | "the portable object will always be in front of any object" (`APPLICAT.CPP:426-427`); `IsCloserThan`'s depth tests come first (`3DOBJECT.CPP:546-560`). |
| B32 | cat drawn over the open panel | slip (NOITE regression) | 1995 ticks, then returns false, then the panel and bar are drawn, then the flush (`GENSCENE.CPP:375-409`, `APPLICAT.CPP:270-287`); a panel redraw in `TreatCollision` is commented out (`3DOBJECT.CPP:703-706`). |
| B33 | cockroach missing on return | not-covered | 1995 rebuilds `Static=` objects on each scene init (`GENSCENE.CPP:186-204`, with `DefStatic`) and has no "already global" path. |
| B35 | two poses at a walk start or stop | not-covered | The 1995 `SetCurrentSprite` path differs; the exit tick does order the player and exit by `IsCloserThan` (`3DOBJECT.CPP:966-983`, "TreatCollision does not work because the player is not in any list"). |
| B36 | item released just above its rest never stops | same-in-1995, likely a slip | Double half-width (`APPLICAT.CPP:389,396` plus `OBJECT.CPP:794-798`); `PORTAB_DELTA_X 0` (`GAMES.HPP:81`) against `PORTAB_DELTAX 10` for the hand-made portables (`CASSETA.HPP:18`); `FinishedMovement` tests x only (`OBJECT.CPP:665-675`). |
| B37 | two doors on the same tick | same-in-1995 | The exit posts its index (`3DOBJECT.CPP:1020-1028`); static `Exiting` flags; 0x401 re-arms (`GENSCENE.CPP:335-339`). |
| B39 | feet show in the panel slots | same-in-1995 | Panel timer: panel frame, then the player's tick or put, then panel `Put`, then flush (`APPLICAT.CPP:209-237`). The player is drawn under the key-coloured slots. |
| B43 | falling item continues into the next scene | same-in-1995 | `static portable *Selected` is ticked against `CurrentScene` (`APPLICAT.CPP:34,259-267`) and is not reset by `SetCurrentScene`. |
| B48 | closing panel cut around the player | same-in-1995 | `InitAnim` sets `CurrentFrame = 0` for both directions; the down slide uses `PreviousFrame` (`CTRPANEL.CPP:171-200`). |
| B49 | icons vanish while the panel slides | intended | `if( On && !Animating ) PutObjects(...)` (`CTRPANEL.CPP:100-114`). |
| B50 | releasing one arrow stops the walk | same-in-1995 | Static `InKey*` flags; any arrow key-up calls `EndMove` (`APPLICAT.CPP:26-160`). |
| B52 | player pixels stay on the Mona Lisa | same-in-1995 for moveables; slip for scene objects | `OBJECT.CPP:219-224` (transparent restore for moveables) against `:152-165` (opaque restore for objects, the transparent version commented out). |
| B53 | sprite holes (index 11) | not-covered (data); the key's history is | `TransparentColor = 11; //6` (`GAMES.HPP:43`), 10 in `GAMEDEFS.HPP:55`, 20 in `DEMO.C:60`. |
| Harmless: objects deleted through the virtual-base pointer | | same-in-1995 | `~object` is not virtual (`OBJECT.HPP:921`); `~scene` does `delete Prev` on `object*` (`SCENE.CPP:42-48`). |
| Harmless: RealizePalette result uninitialised | | slip (NOITE regression) | 1995 calls `RealizePalette` unconditionally, so `f` is always set; only `SelectPalette` sits under the `if` (`GENGAME.CPP:413-429`). |
| Harmless: loadObjects keeps x/y | | not-covered | No saves in 1995. |
| U1 | status-bar text never drawn | same-in-1995 | `status_bar::Update` exists (`CTRPANEL.CPP:44-62`) and nothing calls it. |
| U2 | control-panel message line never filled | slip (a working 1995 feature was dropped) | `SetText(Name)` on `WM_INITSCENE` (`GENSCENE.CPP:331`); drawn by `control_panel::Put` → `Update` (`CTRPANEL.CPP:100-167`). |
| U3 | clearWidth/clearHeight never set | same-in-1995 | `PatBlt(..., DefaultScreenWidth, DefaultScreenHeight, ...)`; `SetScreenDimensions` is never called (`APPLICAT.HPP:114,162`). |
| L1, L2 | RLE blitter | not-covered | No RLE in 1995; only `xlatRle8/4` in UTILS. |
| L3 | base game_init resets the panel before creating it | slip (NOITE regression) | 1995 `application::Init` creates the panel, then `Init`s it, with no reset (`APPLICAT.HPP:122-126`). |
| L4 | draw-list invalidate recursion | slip (tree code reused on a list) | `object_tree::Show` (`VTREE.CPP:202-214`) is right for the tree and wrong for `object_ordlist`. |
| L5 | remove from a full draw list | same-in-1995 | `New->l = Node` unguarded (`VTREE.CPP:369-374`). |
| L6 | message-box buffer overrun | same-in-1995 | `static char Out[100]` + `sprintf` (`GENGAME.CPP:472-474`); the same long `MAX_OBJECT_QTY` text (`OBJECT.HPP:1828`). |
| L7 | background larger than 1295×1039 | same-in-1995 | `MAX_Y 1024`, `MAX_X 1280`, a message box and no return (`SCREEN.HPP:20-24,132-140`). |
| L8 | fourth closet pick | not-covered | |
| L9 | exit index == exitCount | same-in-1995, slip | `ExitNum <= NumExits` (`GENSCENE.CPP:58`) against `Exit()`'s `<` (`SCENE.HPP:201`); `VetExits[NumExits++]` unchecked (`GENSCENE.CPP:457-459`). 1995's `MAX_EXITS` is 10 (`SCENE.HPP:11`); NOITE has 25. |
| L10 | perspective divisors not checked | same-in-1995 | `3DSCREEN.CPP:26-46`. |
| L11 | vacating an unoccupied cell | intended for walls, slip for 0 | `DecrOccup` `else ++` mirrors `IncrOccup`'s negative wall counting (`SCREEN.HPP:77-108`, `SCREEN.CPP:118-122`). `Disappear` does not test `IsVisible` (`OBJECT.HPP:553-562`), so double vacates are possible in 1995 too. |
| E*, T*, S*, X*, K*, I*, H*, P*, J* | minigame bugs | not-covered | The minigames are later builds of this engine (see the last section), but none of their game code is in the 1995 source. |



### Other behaviour insights

- **1995 click-exit bug, fixed in NOITE.** `gen_scene::MessageHandling` takes a click on an `IsClickExit` scene
  with `Game->SetCurrentScene(hwnd, (int) wParam)` (`GENSCENE.CPP:344-355`). There `wParam` is the mouse key
  state (MK_LBUTTON = 1, MK_RBUTTON = 2), not an exit index. NOITE posts the animation's own exit index
  (`src/scene.zig` `clickExit`).
- **The player is deliberately kept out of the grid and the draw list.**
  - `_3D_player::OccupScreen`/`DesoccupScreen` are empty (`3DOBJECT.HPP:688-695`).
  - The exit tick's comment says "TreatCollision ... does not work because the player is not in any list"
    (`3DOBJECT.CPP:966`).
  - This is why the walking player cannot trigger B29, and why the panel and player redraws are special cases.
- **Draw-list order.** `object_ordlist::Insert` walks past every node the new object `IsCloserThan` and stops at the
  first it is not (`VTREE.CPP:252-268`). The list runs far to near, but the comparator is not transitive, so the
  order depends on insertion order (B31, B40).
- **Exit key objects and alternative exits** go back to 1995. When its animation ends, an exit posts
  `WM_EXITSCENE` with `ExitNum` if `HasKey()`, else with `AlternativeExitNum` (`3DOBJECT.CPP:1018-1028`;
  `DefAltExit`, `GENSCENE.CPP:466-474`). This is where `KeyObjectName` and `2Exit=` come from.
- **Reach and pick-up rules.** Only exits need the player within 1.5 player hit widths
  (`APPLICAT.CPP:325`). The same test for portables and animations is commented out (`:339`, `:360`). A pick-up
  needs only a non-full inventory (`MAX_OBJECT_PORT 8`, `OBJECT.HPP:15`).
- **Panel thresholds.** In 1995 the panel slides up at y > 440 and down at y < 340 when not dragging
  (`APPLICAT.CPP:176-199`). The inventory slots are at x = 92 + 56·i, y = panel + 70 (`CTRPANEL.CPP:283-324`).
- **Corrigida's cursor addition had a 1995 precedent.** `POLAR.CPP:73-79,130-146` loads an `IDC_SIZEWE` "exit"
  cursor and swaps the class cursor over the exits, all commented out. `ped1/` ships `HAND.CUR`, `LEFT.CUR`
  and `RIGHT.CUR`. Exit-hover and hand cursors were planned and dropped.
- **Debug aids worth porting as a dev overlay.**
  - `DrawSolid` draws each object's 3D box (`3DOBJECT.CPP:84-152`; called every tick in `POLAR.CPP` and
    `BARATA.CPP`).
  - `_3D_moveable::Show` writes `"(%03d,%03d)=>(%03d,%03d,%03d)"` 2D→3D coordinates to the status bar
    (`3DOBJECT.CPP:409-419`). That is U1's only caller in NOITE.
- **The "unused mover/backdrop subclass remains"** in docs/DESIGN.md are `bg_layer` (`BGLAYER.HPP`) and
  `parallax_screen`: up to `MAX_LAYERS` 5 scrolling background layers (`3DSCREEN.HPP:60`,
  `3DSCREEN.CPP:48-110`), never used by the INI engine.
- **Timers and steps of the hand-written 1995 demo** (`CASSETA.HPP:3-22`):
  - control panel: timer 2, 55 ms;
  - player: timer 3, 100 ms, step 25×10 (`ZEMANE_DELTAX 25 // 12 // 37 // 60 // 20`, tuned by hand);
  - doors: timer 4, 300 ms;
  - portables: timer 5, 55 ms.

  NOITE takes these from the INI instead. `CTRPANEL.CPP:125` gives the panel message colour RGB(255,255,0).
- **The 1995 handlers show the per-scene state pattern NOITE kept.** File-level flags such as `FPe`, `FPolar`,
  `JaPintei` ("checks whether the background was already painted") and `FIsSplashed` ("tomatoes squashed only
  once") gate animations, with Portuguese comments. `BARATA.CPP:121,169` arms `FREEZE_TIMER` with
  `SetSystemTimer` but kills it with `KillTimer`, an API mismatch. The same family of timer slips leads to B21.
- **ped1 → demog changes** (one week apart): the arrow keys moved to `_3D_game_player`. During the panel timer, a
  player that is not moving now animates instead of only being put (`APPLICAT.CPP:215-226`). `GENSCENE` added
  `Init`/`Set3DPosition`/`TreatCollision` for every INI object and `DefExit(AutoExit)`.
- **Correction to a helper report:** READDATA *is* used in 1995. `gen_scene::Init` and `gen_applic` call
  `SeekScene`/`ReadScreen`/`ReadLimits`/`ReadObject`/`ReadExit`… (`GENSCENE.CPP:89-258`, `GENAPLIC.CPP:163`), and
  `DEMO.MAK` links them. Only READDATA's own test `WinMain` is commented out.



### INI keys then vs now

Each key is parsed with `SearchLine` + `sscanf`, checked against the number of conversions, with no defaults.
`%s` stops at white space, which is why every name has a trailing space. The key's first letter is matched
case-sensitively, then the whole key with `stricmp`. `demog/` and `ped1/` READDATA.CPP are identical.

| Key | 1995 fields (`READDATA.CPP`) | ped1 INI | shipped INI | change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FirstScene` | 6 (`:528-553`) | yes | yes (6) | none |
| `Player` | 14 (`:418-447`) | yes | yes (15) | `Lifes` added |
| `Sprite` | 7 ×NumSprites | ×5 | ×5 (7) | none |
| `ControlPanel` | 8 (`:475-500`) | yes | renamed `Control` (8) | renamed; the error text still says ControlPanel |
| `StatusBar` | 6 | yes | yes (6) | none |
| `Portable` | 19 (incl. `_3Dz0`) | ×13 | ×48 (22) | 3 ints added; `InitialScene` changed from a room name (`Sala`) to a section name (`1fsala`) |
| `Screen` | 15 | yes | ×116 (18) | `_MayDropObject, Is_1Animation, Is_ClickExit` moved here from `Exit=` |
| `Limits` | 8 | yes | ×116 (8) | none; field names in the "Top 10" item 10 |
| `Local` | 15 | yes | ×74 (15) | none |
| `Animated` | 18 | yes | ×86 (20) | `NumFrames` moved after `PerTimer`; `NomeWAVE` added |
| `Static` | 18 | comment only | ×3 (19) | `NumFrames` moved; `NomeWAVE` added |
| `Exit` | 26, ending `…,Is_1Animation,Is_ClickExit` | yes | ×113 (27) | tail is now `_Is_AVI,KeyObjectName,NumFrames,NomeWAVE` (`assets/CASSETA.INI:96-98`) |
| `WAVE`, `ZoomPort`, `Moveable`, `2Exit`, `Control` | none | none | 444 / 9 / 7 / 2 / 1 | new since 1995 |

Other changes:
- **Sections.** ped1 has `[Casseta]`, rooms named after the room (`[QuartoZe]`, `[Gavboy1-3]`, `[Corredor]`) and
  `[Fim]`, 6 sections against a fixed table of 40 (`READDATA.CPP:15-19`). The shipped file has 118 sections named
  after the background BMP (`[1fquarbo]`…), plus `[salvar2]`, `[fundinic]`, `[creditos]` and `[fim]`.
- **Oct 1995 data errors that the count check misses.** `ped1/CASSETA.INI:73` `Exit=23..26` carry two extra ints,
  so `%s` swallows `6,200,quartoze` and the count is still 26. `:97` `Exit=27` lacks X, which is a fatal error.
- **Comments that explain keys.**
  - `READDATA.CPP:321` turns a missing key from an error into a silent FALSE.
  - `:355` "lê qual é o drive do cd-rom" ("reads which drive the CD-ROM is") is a stub; `CASSETUP.INI`'s
    `DriveCD`/`DriveHD` are post-1995.
  - `MontaNome` (`:391`) builds `%s.bmp` or `%s%d.bmp`. The shipped engine adds the `.avi`, `.wav` and `.rle` kinds.
  - `SkipSeparators` silently swallows end of file (`:193-196`).
- **The port's reader matches the 1995 algorithm**, including the first-letter cutoff and the count-only check.
  No port quirk traces back to a parsing difference.



### Owner questions

All 8 open owner questions are minigame gameplay or Windows-timing questions. The 1995 source settles none of them.

| doc:line | question | settled by 1995? |
|---|---|---|
| docs/inv/DESIGN.md:667 | I2: should Corrigida hold the cannon for the whole 420 ms explosion (now in `all`), or keep the cut-short explosion as a choice? | not covered |
| docs/salgin/DESIGN.md:702 (and :164) | Should lasting the 90 s clock count as a win when behind on energy (SALGIN and XILINDRO)? | not covered |
| docs/jardim/DESIGN.md:736 | J6: a blocked step ends the held walk, or one hit per contact, or one life per scream? | not covered |
| docs/jardim/DESIGN.md:739 | J7: fix the 0x40b/0x40c handshake in JARDIM or in NOITE (`espquete`)? Is `espanf` in the first Corrigida ending wanted? | not covered (no minigame handshake in 1995) |
| docs/jardim/DESIGN.md:743 | J4: keep the per-wall texture fix in Corrigida, or the original look? | not covered |
| docs/jardim/DESIGN.md:745 | J5 in Original: panic where the original crashes, or draw silently in both versions? | not covered |
| docs/jardim/DESIGN.md:747 (and :582) | Keep the queue's exact 50/55 ms periods, or quantise to Windows 3.1's 55 ms tick (all programs)? | not covered. Windows behaviour; 1995 only shows `REFRESH_PERIOD 50` and 55 ms object periods (`GAMES.HPP:67`, `CASSETA.HPP:4,22`) |

The "unverified" notes in docs/DESIGN.md:336 (timer ordering and queue capacity), :438 (MCI resume) and
docs/encaixe/DESIGN.md:250 (cross-task activation order) are about Windows or MCI, so the source cannot settle
them either. The 1995 symbols *do* settle several `unk` fields (item 1 above).



## Minigames sharing the engine


What the docs say:
- ENCAIXE is NOITE's engine rebuilt in the small model.
- TIRO is built from the same sources (`/Od`).
- INV and SERIAL are built on TIRO.
- SALGIN and XILINDRO are one pair copied from TIRO.
- INFERNO is TIRO's engine at nearly the same version, in the large model.
- SAUNA and JARDIM are later builds with NOITE's layouts.

All of them are derivatives of the 1995 class library, and none is a separate engine:
- **Same vtable shapes.** The Game vtable has 7 slots (`application`), the Scene vtable 13 (`scene`), ControlPanel
  9 (`Init1SpriteAnim` + `control_panel`'s 8), Object3D 8 (`Find_z, IsOn, IsOver, IsCloserThan, DetectedCollision,
  SetPosition(x,y,screen,z), Set3DPosition` …), Condition 3 (`HasOccurred/Occur/Reset`), Mover 1
  (`moveable::Paint`). SceneObject follows `object`'s slot order. The 18 type tests are 1995's
  `IsPlayer..ToPortable`, and `IsClass1..5` are each game's own type tests (INV isShot..isBomb, INFERNO
  isBig..isPowerUp, TIRO/SERIAL isProjectile/isTarget/isCrossbow, SALGIN/XILINDRO isFighter/isOpponent).
- **Same field order.** SceneObject is `object`: Next/Prev/List/Layer/X/Y/Name/PaletteOffSet/NumColors/Sprites/…/
  BufferHeader/Orientation/IsVisible/Initialized/CollideObject/InCollision/InShowQueue/PosShowQueue/AuxX/AuxY/
  Dirty*/LastDirty*/_SceneName. Mover is `moveable` (OldX 0x16, NewX 0x1A, DeltaX 0x1E, Bresenham
  Dist/IncrNE/IncrE/IncrN, Timer1/Period/Moving 0x30-0x34). OccupancyGrid is `occup_matrix` (0x10 bytes) plus the
  global `Mat[65][81]`/`Obj[65][81]`. DrawList is `object_ordlist` (100 nodes, New, First). Game is `application`
  (CurrentScene, LastScene, StatusBar, ControlPanel, Player, Portables, DirtyObjects, VisibleObjects).
- **Builds:**
  - ENCAIXE, TIRO, INV, SERIAL, SALGIN and XILINDRO are small or medium model, have no `SceneName` slot and no
    `_SceneName` field, and are closest to 1995. ENCAIXE even keeps both `DrawSolid` overloads, and its update
    list holds 30 entries = `MAX_OBJECT_QTY`.
  - INFERNO is TIRO's version in the large model, with exactly 1995's far offsets. Its slot 4C "getSceneName"
    sits where 1995 has `TreatCollision`, so that doc name is probably wrong.
  - SAUNA and JARDIM are NOITE's revision (the 4 leading RLE slots, sceneName at +0x478). JARDIM's raycaster
    has no 1995 counterpart.
- **Blitters.** NOITE segment 1000's two 32-bit routines (`dib_rows_copy_transparent32` at 0024,
  `dib_rows_copy32` at 0114) are the WinG sample's `TransCopyDIBBits`/`CopyDIBBits` (FAST32.ASM): NE segment 1
  is byte-identical in DEMO.EXE and NOITE.EXE. Any minigame with the same pair inherits B12.


## Next steps

1. **Import DEMO.EXE into Ghidra as a twin program** (`ref/src1995/ped1/DEMO.EXE`, x86 16-bit
   protected mode).
   - Ghidra's PDB/CodeView loaders do not read NB09 from an NE file. Apply the names with a
     script fed by `symbols.txt` instead: procedures, globals, and the class layouts as
     structs.
   - With the twin in the project, Version Tracking or the BSim/function-ID tools can confirm
     or correct the confidence-5/6 matches. DEMO is `/Od` and NOITE optimised, so expect
     structural rather than exact matches.
2. **A Ghidra rename pass on NOITE.EXE**, after the owner reviews the proposed names. Start
   with the confidence 8–9 rows.
   - The 25 `FUN_*` functions that get a name, among them `FUN_1010_1720` = `object::Save`,
     `FUN_1010_b10c` = `object::Refresh`, `FUN_1010_13e6` = `object::Find_z`, the dead
     `bg_layer` methods at 1010:a8c0–a92a, and `IsClass1..5` at 1010:1d12–1d42.
   - The 22 `unk*` struct fields: `layer`, `inCollision`, Mover's `xSpeed…yMin` and
     `counter`, Backdrop's `paletteOffSet`/`numColors`.
   - The INI globals `g_iniArg816C` → `g_iniCor` and `g_iniLimits1..8` → the
     `LinhaSup…DeltaLatDir` names.
   - Plate comments citing `ref/src1995/<FILE>:<line>`.

   Whether to adopt the `xx_` module prefixes for these C++ methods, or keep the current
   class-style names (`scene_object_*`), is the owner's call. The mapping supports either.
3. **Fix the docs that the source contradicts.**
   - CLAUDE.md and the minigame QUALITY.md files call segment 1000 the "transrle.cpp
     blitters". It is FAST32.ASM, and this commit corrects CLAUDE.md. `transrle.cpp` is a
     later RLE module whose only NOITE traces are `rle_blit_transparent` and an unreferenced
     assert string in 1018.
   - CLAUDE.md's "Inferred: the handler index is the scene number" is confirmed by
     GENSCENE.CPP's `VetMessageHandling[SceneNumber]`.
   - INFERNO's slot 4C `getSceneName` is probably `TreatCollision`.
4. **BUGS.md:** add the 1995 evidence to the entries listed in "BUGS.md entries vs 1995
   source", especially B32 and B52 (NOITE regressions), L11 (half deliberate), and B12/B13
   (Microsoft sample code). Each entry needs the owner's agreement before its verdict changes.
